(c) every minute named, or the remainder reported with what it is and why it could not be booked, never a silent "other"; (d) the report names the biggest blocking buckets and whether each could run in parallel ("the things that take a larger percentage of our time seem like they are worth asking whether they can be parallelized"); (e) review split by kind - the review commands into plan review (decide, plan-delta, plan-verdict), claims round, record and pop-up checks, and arbiter, each with its agents' own minutes beside its command minutes; (f) agents' time by what they do, never pooled ("we do want the spec fidelity agents, reading and judging, to be in a separate category than the reading and writing of code, because that is conceptually different, and the way that we would optimize it is also different"): code builders' model turns (reading code, writing code, running) apart from review agents' reading and judging, split by kind - plan review (spec-fidelity), claims round (claims-triage, impl-drift), record and pop-up checks, arbiter, research and page sessions - each with minutes, blocking minutes and percentages; (g) per make target ("each makefile command that we run will have its own timing breakdown"): a table of every target run in the window - runs, minutes, blocking minutes, both percentages, the bucket it books to - beside the category table, a Bash call that runs no make target listed by its program (cargo, the hamlet binary, a sleep or wait loop) so nothing hides in "other"; (i) waiting on the GM out of the other percentages ("time spent waiting for me should probably not be counted in those percentages, even if it is recorded"): reported with its share of the whole, every other row's percentages of the working time, the whole less the GM's, the header naming each column's denominator; (j) "waiting: nothing in flight" broken down on every run ("as long as that automatically gets broken down in the future"): each gap classed by what came just before and after it, each class its own row, the rest named unexplained; (k) blocking only what was waited on ("if they were running in parallel with other things ... that number would be misleading"): a page session's time is blocking only where nothing else its waiter waited on was in flight, and the window's minutes with 0, 1, 2 and 3 or more actors working at once are reported; (l) blocking by dependency, replacing (k)'s reading (the GM 2026-11-25: "a minute should count as blocking. When something else is waiting on it to be done before it can be started ... that research would be blocking even if there was a different unrelated task happening in a different subagent at the same time ... it doesn't have to be 100% exact"): each piece booked to the task whose next step waited on it, by a clue recorded with the data - a dispatch's or page brief's `Blocks: <feature> T<n>` / `Blocks: none` line, a command's `L7R_BLOCKS=` - else by stated defaults (a page session or research pass blocks nothing), work blocking no task named apart; the window's blocking is the sum over tasks and may pass the clock; the `Blocks:` line added to the builder brief's STOP POINT block and the page brief's rules
